The paper introduces Instruction-Augmented Long-Horizon Planning (IALP), a system designed to enable humanoid robots to perform complex mobile manipulation tasks in real-world environments. IALP leverages the abstract reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) and integrates them with grounding mechanisms like voxel mapping and grasping pose generation. The system augments user instructions with task-relevant knowledge extracted from both promptable LLM-based predicates and grounding mechanisms, converting this information into PDDL problems. It then employs an LLM planner in a closed-loop interaction to generate feasible and optimal actions based on real-time sensor feedback. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of IALP on several real-world long-horizon tasks, showcasing its ability to achieve high success rates with skills such as navigation, picking, and placing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper lies in its integrated approach to long-horizon planning. While previous works have explored LLMs for task planning or relied heavily on prompt engineering or simulated environments, this research combines LLMs with explicit grounding mechanisms in a closed-loop interactive system. This represents a significant step toward bridging the gap between abstract symbolic planning and real-world robotic execution. A few of the individual components are not novel (LLMs, PDDL, GraspNet, etc.), but the integration and application to the specific problem are where the novelty arises.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to enhance the autonomy of robots operating in unstructured environments. By incorporating real-time sensor feedback and grounding mechanisms, the IALP system addresses the limitations of existing LLM-based planners, which often struggle with real-world constraints and object manipulation. The system also provides a flexible and extendable framework, which facilitates the integration of other grounding mechanisms and modalities. The reported success rates on challenging long-horizon tasks indicate that the approach has practical value for real-world robotics applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Integration of LLMs and Grounding:** The key strength is effectively combining the reasoning of LLMs with grounded perception, addressing a major limitation in prior LLM-based planning approaches.
    *   **Closed-Loop Interaction:**  The closed-loop architecture allows the robot to adapt to unforeseen situations and recover from errors, enhancing the system's robustness.
    *   **Real-World Validation:** Demonstrating the system's performance on real-world robotic tasks with multiple manipulation skills is a strong validation of its practical relevance.
    *   **Extendability:** The described architecture emphasizes the modularity and adaptability of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on Primitive Actions:** The system still depends on a pre-defined library of primitive actions, which can limit the complexity and adaptability of the plans.  The quality of these primitives greatly influences the quality of the overall task execution.  Generalizing the framework to learn novel skills would further enhance its capabilities.
    *   **LLM Reliance:**  The system is heavily reliant on the prompt engineering to extract useful information from LLMs, as it is not able to extract data without proper guidance. Additionally, the selection of prompts is not adaptive, which can potentially benefit the approach further.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The experiments are conducted in a single environment with a limited set of tasks. While the results are promising, further evaluation in diverse settings is needed.
    *   **Error Analysis:** While the overall failure cases were mentioned, a detailed analysis of the types of errors encountered in each task would further strengthen the paper. How often did navigation vs. manipulation errors occur?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of embodied AI by promoting the integration of LLMs and grounding mechanisms for robotic task planning. The IALP system can serve as a valuable framework for researchers working on developing more autonomous and capable robots that can operate in real-world environments.
*   **Rigor:** The experimental methodology and analysis of results are satisfactory but could be enhanced by more task diversity and a deeper dive into error types.

**Overall:**

While IALP represents a compelling approach to long-horizon planning in robotics, some areas require refinement to increase its practicality. In the long term, the integration of grounding mechanisms with LLMs shows significant potential for advancing embodied AI.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel integration of LLMs and grounding mechanisms, demonstrating its effectiveness on real-world robotic tasks. However, the reliance on primitive actions and the limited scope of the experiments prevent it from achieving a higher score. Future work addressing these weaknesses could significantly enhance the impact of the research.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### MegaSR: Mining Customized Semantics and Expressive Guidance for Image Super-Resolution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08096v1)
- **Authors**: Xinrui Li, Jianlong Wu, Xinchuan Huang, Chong Chen, Weili Guan, Xian-Sheng Hua, Liqiang Nie
- **Abstract**: Pioneering text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models have ushered in a new era of real-world image super-resolution (Real-ISR), significantly enhancing the visual perception of reconstructed images. However, existing methods typically integrate uniform abstract textual semantics across all blocks, overlooking the distinct semantic requirements at different depths and the fine-grained, concrete semantics inherently present in the images themselves. Moreover, relying solely on a single type of guidance further disrupts the consistency of reconstruction. To address these issues, we propose MegaSR, a novel framework that mines customized block-wise semantics and expressive guidance for diffusion-based ISR. Compared to uniform textual semantics, MegaSR enables flexible adaptation to multi-granularity semantic awareness by dynamically incorporating image attributes at each block. Furthermore, we experimentally identify HED edge maps, depth maps, and segmentation maps as the most expressive guidance, and propose a multi-stage aggregation strategy to modulate them into the T2I models. Extensive experiments demonstrate the superiority of MegaSR in terms of semantic richness and structural consistency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the MegaSR paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MegaSR, a novel framework for real-world image super-resolution (ISR) based on text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models. MegaSR addresses limitations in existing methods by customizing semantics at the block level within the diffusion U-Net and by integrating multiple forms of expressive guidance. It mines customized block-wise semantics using a proposed Customized Image Semantic Mining Module (CISM²) and employs a Multi-Stage Guidance Aggregation Module (MS-GAM) to fuse HED edge maps, depth maps, and segmentation maps effectively.  The paper claims improved semantic richness and structural consistency in reconstructed images, supported by extensive experimental results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of block-wise customized semantics and the multi-stage guidance aggregation approach.  Customizing semantic inputs at different depths within the diffusion U-Net is a valid and potentially impactful idea, especially considering the varying roles of different U-Net blocks. The identification of HED edge maps, depth maps, and segmentation maps as expressive guidance signals and their structured fusion via MS-GAM also represents a novel contribution.  However, the individual components (diffusion models, semantic segmentation, depth estimation) are not novel; the contribution is primarily in how they are integrated and adapted for the ISR task.

*   **Significance:**  ISR is a well-established field, and improving the perceptual quality and structural consistency of super-resolved images is a significant goal. MegaSR demonstrates improved performance in this regard, particularly as evidenced by non-reference metrics like MANIQA, MUSIQ, and CLIPIQA. This indicates that the generated images are more visually appealing and natural to human observers.  The work could potentially influence future research on diffusion-based ISR by highlighting the importance of customized semantics and multi-faceted guidance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The motivation for customized semantics and multi-stage guidance is well-articulated and empirically supported (Figure 1).
    *   The proposed CISM² module is simple yet effective.
    *   MS-GAM effectively leverages complementary guidance signals for better structure and detail preservation.
    *   Extensive experimental results demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods, especially concerning perceptual quality.
    *   Clear and well-structured paper with informative figures.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on external models (DAGECLIP, HED, MaskDINO, DepthAnythingV2, CLIP) for guidance signals could be a limitation, as the performance of MegaSR is dependent on the quality of these pre-trained models. While fine-tuning helps, their inherent biases and errors could still propagate.
    *   While the ablation studies are helpful, a more detailed analysis of the computational cost and efficiency of the proposed modules would be beneficial.
    *   The paper touches upon the limitations of DAGEs under extreme degradation but doesn't offer concrete solutions to address this. Future work should consider more robust or adaptive degradation-aware extractors.
    *   The qualitative results, while visually appealing, are still subject to subjective interpretation.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper presents a compelling case for the importance of customized semantics and multi-faceted guidance in diffusion-based ISR. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, the proposed MegaSR framework effectively integrates them to achieve state-of-the-art performance. The improvements in perceptual quality, as measured by non-reference metrics, are particularly significant. However, the reliance on external models and the lack of a thorough computational analysis are limitations. Considering all these factors, the contribution is substantial but not transformative.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AI-native Memory 2.0: Second Me
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08102v1)
- **Authors**: Jiale Wei, Xiang Ying, Tao Gao, Felix Tao, Jingbo Shang
- **Abstract**: Human interaction with the external world fundamentally involves the exchange of personal memory, whether with other individuals, websites, applications, or, in the future, AI agents. A significant portion of this interaction is redundant, requiring users to repeatedly provide the same information across different contexts. Existing solutions, such as browser-stored credentials, autofill mechanisms, and unified authentication systems, have aimed to mitigate this redundancy by serving as intermediaries that store and retrieve commonly used user data. The advent of large language models (LLMs) presents an opportunity to redefine memory management through an AI-native paradigm: SECOND ME. SECOND ME acts as an intelligent, persistent memory offload system that retains, organizes, and dynamically utilizes user-specific knowledge. By serving as an intermediary in user interactions, it can autonomously generate context-aware responses, prefill required information, and facilitate seamless communication with external systems, significantly reducing cognitive load and interaction friction. Unlike traditional memory storage solutions, SECOND ME extends beyond static data retention by leveraging LLM-based memory parameterization. This enables structured organization, contextual reasoning, and adaptive knowledge retrieval, facilitating a more systematic and intelligent approach to memory management. As AI-driven personal agents like SECOND ME become increasingly integrated into digital ecosystems, SECOND ME further represents a critical step toward augmenting human-world interaction with persistent, contextually aware, and self-optimizing memory systems. We have open-sourced the fully localizable deployment system at GitHub: https://github.com/Mindverse/Second-Me.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AI-native Memory 2.0: Second Me":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Second Me," an AI-native memory system designed to offload, organize, and dynamically utilize user-specific knowledge. Unlike traditional memory solutions (like autofill), Second Me leverages large language models (LLMs) for structured organization, contextual reasoning, and adaptive retrieval. It acts as an intermediary between users and the external world, autonomously generating context-aware responses and facilitating seamless communication. The system builds upon the authors' previous work on Large Personal Models (LPMs) and is structured in three layers: a raw data layer (L0), a natural language memory layer (L1), and an AI-native memory layer (L2). The authors focus on improving the L2 layer through diverse data sources, supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and direct preference optimization (DPO). They define three key tasks: memory-based Q&A, context completion, and context critique. The paper details a fully automated training pipeline, evaluation metrics, and preliminary experimental results. Finally, the authors discuss potential applications and have open-sourced their deployment system.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents an interesting and potentially valuable contribution to the field of personalized AI and memory management. The concept of an AI-native memory system that dynamically adapts to user context is appealing and addresses a real need for reducing cognitive load and improving human-computer interaction.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Concept:** The idea of using LLMs to create a dynamic, context-aware personal memory system goes beyond simple data storage and retrieval.
*   **Well-Defined Architecture:** The three-layer architecture is conceptually clear and provides a good framework for organizing different types of memory.
*   **Automated Training Pipeline:** The development of a fully automated training pipeline is a significant achievement, as it enables personalization at scale while addressing user privacy concerns.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a comprehensive set of tasks and evaluation metrics for assessing the performance of Second Me.
*   **Open-Source Implementation:** Releasing the code as open-source encourages further research and development.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Experimental Results:** While the paper presents promising experimental results, they are preliminary. The evaluation could be more rigorous with a larger and more diverse user base.  Specifically, the human evaluations seem very limited.
*   **Evaluation Bias**: The authors acknowledge a bias in the automatic evaluation towards longer responses. This is a major issue. If unaddressed this means the automated results in the paper are potentially invalid.
*   **Lack of comparative benchmarking:** The paper fails to show the Second Me performance against a strong baseline or traditional approach like RAG (Retrieval Augmented Generation).
*   **Overclaims**: The paper contains a number of overclaims, particularly in the "Applications" section, bordering on buzzword bingo (NFTs, Blockchain, etc.) This detracts from the scientific rigor of the work.
*   **Unclear contribution relative to LPM:** Given that this is "Memory 2.0" and based on the LPM work from last year, there's a lack of clear articulation of the delta of improvements, beyond just a very general description. What are the specific pain points of LPM that Second Me addressed?
*   **"Strong" COT methodology questionable**: Using Deepseek R1 is great, but it is a black box, so it is not fully clear whether the models using this methodology are learning good things or just overfitting on the Deepseek R1 style output.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty of the paper lies in the application of LLMs for dynamic personal memory management and the development of a fully automated training pipeline for personalization. The concept is significant because it addresses the growing need for personalized AI and more efficient human-computer interaction. However, the lack of strong comparative benchmarking and the limited experimental results somewhat diminish the overall impact.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field of personalized AI and memory management by demonstrating the feasibility of using LLMs for dynamic knowledge organization and retrieval. The open-source implementation could also encourage further research and development in this area.

**Score: 6/10**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents an interesting concept and a solid system architecture. The development of an automated training pipeline is a significant achievement. However, the preliminary nature of the experimental results, the limitations of the evaluation setup, the lack of proper benchmarking, and the overstated claims in the applications section prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. The novelty is present, but the impact is limited by the lack of comprehensive validation and strong comparison against existing solutions.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### ACE: Concept Editing in Diffusion Models without Performance Degradation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08116v1)
- **Authors**: Ruipeng Wang, Junfeng Fang, Jiaqi Li, Hao Chen, Jie Shi, Kun Wang, Xiang Wang
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based text-to-image models have demonstrated remarkable capabilities in generating realistic images, but they raise societal and ethical concerns, such as the creation of unsafe content. While concept editing is proposed to address these issues, they often struggle to balance the removal of unsafe concept with maintaining the model's general genera-tive capabilities. In this work, we propose ACE, a new editing method that enhances concept editing in diffusion models. ACE introduces a novel cross null-space projection approach to precisely erase unsafe concept while maintaining the model's ability to generate high-quality, semantically consistent images. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ACE significantly outperforms the advancing baselines,improving semantic consistency by 24.56% and image generation quality by 34.82% on average with only 1% of the time cost. These results highlight the practical utility of concept editing by mitigating its potential risks, paving the way for broader applications in the field. Code is avaliable at https://github.com/littlelittlenine/ACE-zero.git
- **Summary**: Okay, I've reviewed the paper "ACE: Concept Editing in Diffusion Models without Performance Degradation" and will provide a summary, followed by a critical evaluation.

**Summary:**

This paper introduces ACE, a new method for concept editing in diffusion models. The goal is to remove unsafe content (e.g., nudity, copyright infringement, social biases) without significantly degrading the model's general image generation capabilities.  ACE employs a three-step approach: 1) Concept Erasing (finding optimal perturbations to remove unsafe concepts), 2) Null-space Projection (projecting the perturbations onto the null space of normal text representations to preserve model general capabilities), and 3) Cross Null-space Projection (to decouple safe and unsafe concepts following the cross attention layer).  The authors demonstrate that ACE outperforms existing methods in terms of preserving semantic consistency and image quality while maintaining comparable editing performance and reducing time cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies a significant problem in concept editing: the trade-off between removing unsafe content and preserving image quality and semantic coherence.
    *   **Well-Motivated Method:** The use of null-space projection is well-motivated by the need to avoid distorting normal text representations when editing unsafe concepts. The three-step approach is logically structured and addresses the key challenges. It's a smart application of techniques successfully used in LLMs to the domain of diffusion models.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors provide extensive experimental results across multiple datasets and evaluation metrics. The quantitative results (CLIP, FID, LPIPS) consistently show that ACE outperforms baseline methods. The qualitative examples also support the claims regarding semantic consistency and image quality. The consideration of runtime is also crucial and highlights the efficiency gains.
    *   **Good Writing Quality:** The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to understand the proposed method and the experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is novel in this context, each individual component (null-space projection, etc.) has been explored in other domains. The core contribution lies in *how* these components are integrated for diffusion models.
    *   **Limited Scope of "Unsafe" Concepts:** The evaluation, while comprehensive, focuses primarily on a specific set of "unsafe" concepts. It's unclear how well ACE would generalize to other types of editing tasks (e.g., stylistic changes, attribute manipulation).
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While not explicitly discussed, the method likely has some sensitivity to hyperparameter settings (e.g., the dimension of the null space). More analysis of robustness and sensitivity to different settings would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Reliance on Existing Methods:** The first step, "Concept Erasing," relies on UCE or RECE. Thus, ACE's performance is inherently tied to the effectiveness of these initial steps. The paper could be stronger by explicitly addressing the limitations or dependence on specific concept erasure methods.

*   **Significance and Impact:**

    *   The paper addresses a critical issue in the development of diffusion models – safety and controllability.
    *   The proposed method is practical and efficient, making it more likely to be adopted in real-world applications.
    *   The results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods, suggesting that ACE could become a valuable tool for developers and users of diffusion models.
    *   The work could inspire further research on combining different techniques for concept editing and exploring novel ways to balance safety and generative capabilities.
    *   The general framework of projecting perturbations into a null space can provide a lot of other research directions.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of concept editing in diffusion models. The use of null-space projection is well-motivated and effective in preserving image quality while removing unsafe content. While the individual components are not entirely novel, the integrated approach and the comprehensive experimental results make this a valuable contribution. The method's practicality and efficiency are also strong points. Despite the limitation, this paper can inspire more research directions on concept editing and perturbation.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper is valuable but has some incremental novelty. It's well-executed and addresses a pressing problem with a practical solution but depends on underlying methods. It is a solid piece of engineering, but doesn't have the same disruptive potential as a fundamentally new insight. The efficiency and practical utility of ACE push it above a 7, making it a solid contribution that's likely to be influential within the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM4MAC: An LLM-Driven Reinforcement Learning Framework for MAC Protocol Emergence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08123v1)
- **Authors**: Renxuan Tan, Rongpeng Li, Zhifeng Zhao
- **Abstract**: With the advent of 6G systems, emerging hyper-connected ecosystems necessitate agile and adaptive medium access control (MAC) protocols to contend with network dynamics and diverse service requirements. We propose LLM4MAC, a novel framework that harnesses large language models (LLMs) within a reinforcement learning paradigm to drive MAC protocol emergence. By reformulating uplink data transmission scheduling as a semantics-generalized partially observable Markov game (POMG), LLM4MAC encodes network operations in natural language, while proximal policy optimization (PPO) ensures continuous alignment with the evolving network dynamics. A structured identity embedding (SIE) mechanism further enables robust coordination among heterogeneous agents. Extensive simulations demonstrate that on top of a compact LLM, which is purposefully selected to balance performance with resource efficiency, the protocol emerging from LLM4MAC outperforms comparative baselines in throughput and generalization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LLM4MAC: An LLM-Driven Reinforcement Learning Framework for MAC Protocol Emergence" proposes a new framework, LLM4MAC, that combines Large Language Models (LLMs) and Reinforcement Learning (RL) for the automatic design (emergence) of Medium Access Control (MAC) protocols. The key idea is to use LLMs to understand the network state and generate actions (signaling and transmission policies), while RL, specifically Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO), is used to fine-tune the LLM and adapt to changing network dynamics. A structured identity embedding (SIE) mechanism is introduced to facilitate coordination between heterogeneous agents (UEs and Base Station). The paper claims that LLM4MAC outperforms traditional MARL approaches and LLM-based prompt learning in terms of throughput and generalization in a dynamic uplink data transmission scheduling (UDTS) environment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents an interesting approach to MAC protocol design by integrating LLMs and RL. The combination of LLMs for high-level reasoning and RL for fine-grained adaptation is a promising direction. The SIE mechanism appears to be a useful contribution for enabling agent differentiation and coordination within the LLM framework. The re-formulation of the MAC layer scheduling task as a semantics-generalized POMG extends the application of POMGs in wireless communication. However, similar ideas combining LLMs and RL have been explored in other domains, such as robotics and game playing. The novelty lies primarily in the *application* of this combined approach to MAC protocol design and the specific implementation details (SIE, unified policy fusion).

*   **Significance:** Automatically designing MAC protocols is a significant challenge, particularly in dynamic and heterogeneous environments. The LLM4MAC framework has the potential to enable more adaptable and efficient MAC protocols than hand-designed solutions. If the approach scales well to more complex scenarios and larger networks, it could significantly impact the future of wireless communication system design. However, the practicality of deploying such a complex system in real-world scenarios needs to be discussed in more detail. Considerations include the computational overhead of LLMs, the energy consumption of frequent reasoning, and the robustness of the framework to adversarial attacks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper addresses a relevant problem.
    *   The proposed framework is well-structured and motivated.
    *   The SIE mechanism and policy fusion method seem promising.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate improved performance compared to baselines.
    *   The use of a relatively compact LLM (Flan-T5 base) is commendable and shows awareness of resource constraints.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper lacks a detailed discussion of the computational complexity and deployment challenges of LLM4MAC.
    *   The experiments are limited to a simplified UDTS environment.  The generalization ability to more realistic channel models, interference patterns, and mobility scenarios is not fully demonstrated.
    *   The explanation of certain aspects of the implementation, such as the precise prompts used and the mapping functions, could be more thorough.
    *   While the paper compares LLM4MAC to baselines, it does not extensively analyze *why* LLM4MAC outperforms them.  What specific behaviors are learned, and how do these behaviors lead to improved throughput and generalization?
    *   Limited analysis on potential fairness issues among UEs, if some UEs are favored by the learned policies.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper can motivate researchers to investigate LLM-based solutions for wireless communication problems, pushing the boundaries of how AI can improve network performance and adaptability. It also contributes to the emerging field of protocol emergence, where protocols are learned rather than designed. However, the true impact will depend on how well the approach scales and how practical it proves to be in real-world deployments.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a promising approach to MAC protocol design using LLMs and RL. However, further research is needed to address the limitations and explore the full potential of the framework.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates an innovative approach to a significant problem, showing promising initial results. The combination of LLMs and RL, with the added SIE, gives a useful framework for dynamic protocol design. However, it needs more thorough evaluation in more complex and realistic scenarios and greater analysis of the learned behaviors. The discussion of computational complexity and real-world deployment challenges is also lacking. The novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking, given existing work combining LLMs and RL in other domains. Thus, while the paper makes a valuable contribution, it is not without limitations. The significance and impact on the field are therefore rated moderate-to-high, justifying the assigned score.

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Large Scale Multi-Task Bayesian Optimization with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08131v1)
- **Authors**: Yimeng Zeng, Natalie Maus, Haydn Thomas Jones, Jeffrey Tao, Fangping Wan, Marcelo Der Torossian Torres, Cesar de la Fuente-Nunez, Ryan Marcus, Osbert Bastani, Jacob R. Gardner
- **Abstract**: In multi-task Bayesian optimization, the goal is to leverage experience from optimizing existing tasks to improve the efficiency of optimizing new ones. While approaches using multi-task Gaussian processes or deep kernel transfer exist, the performance improvement is marginal when scaling to more than a moderate number of tasks. We introduce a novel approach leveraging large language models (LLMs) to learn from, and improve upon, previous optimization trajectories, scaling to approximately 2000 distinct tasks. Specifically, we propose an iterative framework in which an LLM is fine-tuned using the high quality solutions produced by BayesOpt to generate improved initializations that accelerate convergence for future optimization tasks based on previous search trajectories. We evaluate our method on two distinct domains: database query optimization and antimicrobial peptide design. Results demonstrate that our approach creates a positive feedback loop, where the LLM's generated initializations gradually improve, leading to better optimization performance. As this feedback loop continues, we find that the LLM is eventually able to generate solutions to new tasks in just a few shots that are better than the solutions produced by "from scratch" by Bayesian optimization while simultaneously requiring significantly fewer oracle calls.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BOLT, a novel approach to multi-task Bayesian optimization (BO) that leverages large language models (LLMs) to improve the efficiency of optimizing new tasks. Instead of building task relationships directly into the BO surrogate model, BOLT fine-tunes an LLM on the optimization trajectories of previously solved tasks. This fine-tuned LLM then generates strong initial solutions for new tasks, which are subsequently refined by BO. This creates a self-reinforcing feedback loop where BO enhances the LLM, and the LLM provides better initializations for BO. The approach is validated on database query optimization and antimicrobial peptide design, showing improved performance compared to standard multi-task BO methods and eventually achieving few-shot performance competitive with full BO runs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs for initialization in Bayesian Optimization is novel and addresses a key limitation of traditional multi-task BO methods, which tend to saturate in performance as the number of tasks increases.  The self-reinforcing feedback loop between BO and the LLM is also an interesting concept.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem in various fields, where tasks are similar but not identical.  BOLT offers a potentially scalable solution that can leverage large datasets of past optimization runs. The demonstrated ability to achieve competitive performance with significantly fewer oracle calls has significant implications for cost reduction in real-world applications. The finding that, with enough training data, the LLM can achieve high-quality "one-shot" solutions also has notable practical value.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Scalability:** The paper highlights that BOLT continues to improve as the number of tasks increases, overcoming the saturation limitations observed in traditional multi-task BO.
    *   **Simplicity:** BOLT's architecture and implementation appear relatively straightforward. This makes it potentially easier to adopt and adapt by other researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides strong empirical results on two diverse and challenging domains. The experimental setup is reasonably thorough, and the baselines provide a relevant point of comparison. The ablation studies (self-augmentation, data quality) offer additional insights.
    *   **Generality:** The approach is relatively agnostic to the specific BO implementation, suggesting it can be combined with various advances in the BO literature.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Cost:** The fine-tuning of LLMs can be computationally expensive, although the paper suggests the reduced oracle calls ultimately offset this cost. Further analysis of the total cost (compute time and monetary cost for both BO and LLM fine-tuning) is needed in real world settings.
    *   **Task Representation:** The paper requires a task description context C\[ft] for the LLM prompt, which may not always be readily available or easily defined. The sensitivity of the approach to the quality and informativeness of this context needs further investigation.
    *   **LLM Selection:** The choice of GPT-4-mini-0718 is not thoroughly justified. How would other LLMs affect performance? How would the choice change with task complexity and data availability?
    *   **Reproducibility Concerns:** The reliance on a closed API (OpenAI) might raise some reproducibility concerns. While the authors indicate code release after acceptance, access to GPT-4-mini-0718 for future research depends on OpenAI.

*   **Impact:** If the approach proves to be robust and readily adaptable to other domains, it could significantly impact how Bayesian optimization is applied in practice, particularly in settings involving large numbers of related tasks. The one-shot capability offers substantial practical advantages.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and practically relevant approach to multi-task Bayesian optimization. It overcomes limitations of existing methods by leveraging LLMs in an innovative way. The empirical results demonstrate significant improvements in optimization efficiency and the potential for one-shot performance. While there are weaknesses related to the computational cost of LLM fine-tuning, task representation, and reproducibility, the overall contribution is substantial.

### FlowDPS: Flow-Driven Posterior Sampling for Inverse Problems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08136v1)
- **Authors**: Jeongsol Kim, Bryan Sangwoo Kim, Jong Chul Ye
- **Abstract**: Flow matching is a recent state-of-the-art framework for generative modeling based on ordinary differential equations (ODEs). While closely related to diffusion models, it provides a more general perspective on generative modeling. Although inverse problem solving has been extensively explored using diffusion models, it has not been rigorously examined within the broader context of flow models. Therefore, here we extend the diffusion inverse solvers (DIS) - which perform posterior sampling by combining a denoising diffusion prior with an likelihood gradient - into the flow framework. Specifically, by driving the flow-version of Tweedie's formula, we decompose the flow ODE into two components: one for clean image estimation and the other for noise estimation. By integrating the likelihood gradient and stochastic noise into each component, respectively, we demonstrate that posterior sampling for inverse problem solving can be effectively achieved using flows. Our proposed solver, Flow-Driven Posterior Sampling (FlowDPS), can also be seamlessly integrated into a latent flow model with a transformer architecture. Across four linear inverse problems, we confirm that FlowDPS outperforms state-of-the-art alternatives, all without requiring additional training.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "FlowDPS: Flow-Driven Posterior Sampling for Inverse Problems" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Flow-Driven Posterior Sampling (FlowDPS), a novel flow-based inverse problem solver.  It extends diffusion inverse solvers (DIS) into the flow framework by decomposing the flow Ordinary Differential Equation (ODE) using a flow-version of Tweedie's formula. This decomposition separates the ODE into components for clean image estimation and noise estimation, allowing the integration of likelihood gradients and stochastic noise into these components.  FlowDPS is seamlessly integrated into a latent flow model with a transformer architecture. Experiments across linear inverse problems (super-resolution and deblurring) demonstrate FlowDPS's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art alternatives without requiring additional training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core novelty lies in bridging the gap between flow-based generative models and inverse problem solvers in a principled way, drawing explicit connections to posterior sampling. Decomposing the flow ODE and using a flow-version of Tweedie's formula is a non-trivial contribution.  The connection to Tweedie's formula provides valuable theoretical insights into the relationship between flow models and diffusion models, which are often compared but not always rigorously linked. Leveraging and adapting techniques, like DPS, from the diffusion world into flow models and adapting from decomposed diffusion sampling, shows a good understanding of each world.

*   **Significance:** Addressing inverse problems using generative models is a highly relevant area.  The paper demonstrates a practical solution with strong empirical results and could be very significant. The gains demonstrated against current diffusion and other flow-based inverse solvers highlight the advantages of this approach, as well as the ease of integrating with latent flow models. While the idea of guiding flow models with gradients isn't entirely new (FlowChef), FlowDPS provides a much more principled and effective method rooted in posterior sampling theory.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Rigorous theoretical foundation with the derivation of the flow-version of Tweedie's formula.
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating state-of-the-art performance across various inverse problems and datasets.
    *   Seamless integration into existing flow-based architectures (e.g., Stable Diffusion).
    *   No additional training required, which is a huge practical advantage.
    *   Clear connection to posterior sampling, clarifying the theoretical underpinnings of the approach.
    * Easy code integration with state-of-the-art libraries.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the authors establish theoretical connections, the derivation of proposition 2 could be made more accessible and the assumptions (piecewise linearity) discussed more thoroughly, as this has known limitations.
    *   While the results are generally very strong, the comparison to LatentDAPS and PSLD could be further strengthened by better justifying the selected hyperparameter values. Perhaps an ablation experiment showing the effect of hyperparameters specifically in the flow-based setting would strengthen this result.

*   **Impact and Potential Influence:**  The paper's impact will depend on the adoption of FlowDPS and its influence on subsequent research.  The theoretical insights and strong empirical results suggest that it could become a significant contribution.  The decomposition approach and the way data consistency is enforced could inspire new flow-based inverse problem solvers and potentially influence research on diffusion-based methods.

*The presentation is clear and well written. The overall method produces high-quality results.*

**Justification:**

FlowDPS presents a significant advancement in flow-based generative models for inverse problems, offering a principled and effective approach with strong empirical evidence. While minor improvements in clarity and hyperparameter analysis are possible, the paper's theoretical contributions, practical benefits, and potential for impact justify a high score.

### Bring Remote Sensing Object Detect Into Nature Language Model: Using SFT Method
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08144v1)
- **Authors**: Fei Wang, Chengcheng Chen, Hongyu Chen, Yugang Chang, Weiming Zeng
- **Abstract**: Recently, large language models (LLMs) and visionlanguage models (VLMs) have achieved significant success, demonstrating remarkable capabilities in understanding various images and videos, particularly in classification and detection tasks. However, due to the substantial differences between remote sensing images and conventional optical images, these models face considerable challenges in comprehension, especially in detection tasks. Directly prompting VLMs with detection instructions often fails to yield satisfactory results. To address this issue, this letter explores the application of VLMs for object detection in remote sensing images. Specifically, we utilize publicly available remote sensing object detection datasets, including SSDD, HRSID, and NWPU-VHR-10, to convert traditional annotation information into natural language, thereby constructing an instruction-tuning (SFT) dataset for VLM training. We then evaluate the detection performance of different fine-tuning strategies for VLMs and obtain optimized model weights for object detection in remote sensing images. Finally, we assess the model's prior knowledge capabilities through natural language queries.Experimental results demonstrate that, without modifying the model architecture, remote sensing object detection can be effectively achieved using natural language alone. Additionally, the model exhibits the ability to perform certain vision question answering (VQA) tasks. Our dataset and relevant code will be released soon.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Bring Remote Sensing Object Detect Into Nature Language Model: Using SFT Method" investigates using Vision-Language Models (VLMs) for object detection in remote sensing images.  Given the challenges VLMs face with remote sensing imagery due to its distinct characteristics (grayscale, difference from natural images), the authors focus on fine-tuning a pre-trained VLM (Qwen2.5-VL-7B) using a Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) approach. They create an instruction-tuning dataset by converting annotations from common remote sensing object detection datasets (SSDD, HRSID, NWPU-VHR-10) into natural language format.  The paper explores different fine-tuning strategies, including LoRA with different rank values and the addition of NEFT noise, to optimize performance. The resulting model, RS-OD-Qwen, demonstrates the ability to perform object detection through natural language instructions and retain some vision question answering (VQA) capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates the application of existing large vision and language models (VLMs) to the specialized domain of remote sensing object detection through instruction fine-tuning. While the *specific* implementation of fine-tuning Qwen2.5-VL with LoRA and NEFT noise on remote sensing data is somewhat novel, the *general* approach of instruction tuning VLMs is established. The paper doesn't present any fundamentally new architectures or theoretical insights. While the adaptation to remote sensing is a relevant contribution, the core methodology is not groundbreaking. The approach of adapting existing VLMs to specific domains (like remote sensing) through instruction tuning is becoming more common, and this work follows that trend.
*   **Significance:** The paper has some practical significance. It shows that pre-trained VLMs can be adapted to remote sensing object detection tasks *without* significant architectural modifications, which is valuable because it saves on resource-intensive training. This offers a pathway for leveraging the power of VLMs in areas where data is limited or domain-specific expertise is required. Furthermore, the maintenance of some VQA capabilities is a noteworthy strength. The method supports conversational interfaces and demonstrates that the model retains a degree of general visual understanding. This is significant because traditional, task-specific remote sensing models generally lack these interactive features. The paper's release of the dataset and code makes it potentially useful to other researchers looking to apply VLMs to remote sensing.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined experimental setup.
    *   Demonstration of a working system.
    *   Exploration of different fine-tuning strategies (LoRA with varying ranks, NEFT).
    *   Release of dataset and code.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited novelty of the core method (instruction tuning is well-established).
    *   The performance gains over traditional models are not thoroughly compared (the paper focuses on showing VLMs *can* be used rather than definitively showing they *outperform* task-specific models). It would be beneficial to compare to state-of-the-art remote sensing-specific object detection networks.
    *   Recall is often low, suggesting room for improvement.
    *   Lack of in-depth analysis regarding the model's understanding of remote sensing specific features (e.g. how the model processes SAR characteristics).
    *   It would be valuable to explore the prompt engineering, analyze the prompt sensitivity, and detail the design choices made to improve the performance in specific tasks.
* **Overall Score:**

Score: 6/10

Rationale: While the paper offers a practical demonstration of adapting a VLM for remote sensing object detection using SFT, its novelty is limited by the established nature of instruction tuning. The method is useful but not revolutionary. The significance lies in demonstrating the *feasibility* and retaining some VQA capabilities, making it a relevant but incremental contribution to the field. Further investigation into specific domain-specific adaptations, prompt engineering, and rigorous performance comparison with specialized remote sensing models is required for it to be considered a higher impact work.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### U-StyDiT: Ultra-high Quality Artistic Style Transfer Using Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08157v1)
- **Authors**: Zhanjie Zhang, Ao Ma, Ke Cao, Jing Wang, Shanyuan Liu, Yuhang Ma, Bo Cheng, Dawei Leng, Yuhui Yin
- **Abstract**: Ultra-high quality artistic style transfer refers to repainting an ultra-high quality content image using the style information learned from the style image. Existing artistic style transfer methods can be categorized into style reconstruction-based and content-style disentanglement-based style transfer approaches. Although these methods can generate some artistic stylized images, they still exhibit obvious artifacts and disharmonious patterns, which hinder their ability to produce ultra-high quality artistic stylized images. To address these issues, we propose a novel artistic image style transfer method, U-StyDiT, which is built on transformer-based diffusion (DiT) and learns content-style disentanglement, generating ultra-high quality artistic stylized images. Specifically, we first design a Multi-view Style Modulator (MSM) to learn style information from a style image from local and global perspectives, conditioning U-StyDiT to generate stylized images with the learned style information. Then, we introduce a StyDiT Block to learn content and style conditions simultaneously from a style image. Additionally, we propose an ultra-high quality artistic image dataset, Aes4M, comprising 10 categories, each containing 400,000 style images. This dataset effectively solves the problem that the existing style transfer methods cannot produce high-quality artistic stylized images due to the size of the dataset and the quality of the images in the dataset. Finally, the extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments validate that our U-StyDiT can create higher quality stylized images compared to state-of-the-art artistic style transfer methods. To our knowledge, our proposed method is the first to address the generation of ultra-high quality stylized images using transformer-based diffusion.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces U-StyDiT, a novel approach for ultra-high quality artistic style transfer. The method leverages diffusion transformers (specifically DiT) to repaint a content image using the style information learned from a style image. U-StyDiT proposes a Multi-view Style Modulator (MSM) to extract style information from both local and global perspectives, and a StyDiT Block to learn content and style conditions simultaneously.  To address the scarcity of high-quality artistic style images, the authors also contribute Aes4M, a new dataset of 4 million artistic images across 10 categories, which features improved aesthetic quality, text-image consistency, and clear Canny images.  Experimental results demonstrate that U-StyDiT generates higher quality stylized images compared to existing style transfer methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components. The MSM for multi-view style extraction is a good contribution, as is the StyDiT block. The use of a transformer-based diffusion model for style transfer is not entirely new, but applying it with the MSM and StyDiT block is a valuable improvement. The Aes4M dataset is a significant contribution, addressing a key limitation in the field. The key novelty is the simultaneous learning of style and content conditions on transformer-based diffusion.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important problem in artistic style transfer: generating ultra-high quality stylized images without artifacts.  The proposed U-StyDiT method and the Aes4M dataset have the potential to advance the field significantly. The generated results look superior to those of existing methods, which is also backed by quantitative analysis.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The introduction of the MSM and StyDiT block are well-motivated and contribute to improved style transfer results.
    *   The Aes4M dataset fills a crucial gap in the availability of high-quality artistic style images, thus removing the bottleneck for high-quality outputs.
    *   The qualitative and quantitative results demonstrate the superiority of U-StyDiT over state-of-the-art methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper notes that, "Due to the use of the more powerful FLUX.1-dev [20], which is based on a transformer diffusion structure, our method has certain drawbacks in inference time." This could limit the practical applicability of the method, particularly for real-time applications.
    *   Limited by computational resources, the amount of images used from datasets is lower than the maximum, preventing full exploration of the methods effectiveness with even larger datasets.

*   **Potential Impact:** U-StyDiT has the potential to become a new benchmark for artistic style transfer methods. The Aes4M dataset could be widely adopted by the research community, facilitating further advancements in the field. The improvements contribute to the application of style transfer to practical business scenarios, as well as creative image applications.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:**  The contributions are significant. The MSM and StyDiT blocks, the adoption of transformer-based diffusion, and the construction of Aes4M are substantial improvements over prior work, but the computational expense in inference slightly mitigates these factors. Given the combination of innovation, result quality, and potential to impact the community, a score reflecting significant advancement but acknowledging limitations is warranted.

### Concept-Driven Deep Learning for Enhanced Protein-Specific Molecular Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08160v1)
- **Authors**: Taojie Kuang, Qianli Ma, Athanasios V. Vasilakos, Yu Wang, Qiang, Cheng, Zhixiang Ren
- **Abstract**: In recent years, deep learning techniques have made significant strides in molecular generation for specific targets, driving advancements in drug discovery. However, existing molecular generation methods present significant limitations: those operating at the atomic level often lack synthetic feasibility, drug-likeness, and interpretability, while fragment-based approaches frequently overlook comprehensive factors that influence protein-molecule interactions. To address these challenges, we propose a novel fragment-based molecular generation framework tailored for specific proteins. Our method begins by constructing a protein subpocket and molecular arm concept-based neural network, which systematically integrates interaction force information and geometric complementarity to sample molecular arms for specific protein subpockets. Subsequently, we introduce a diffusion model to generate molecular backbones that connect these arms, ensuring structural integrity and chemical diversity. Our approach significantly improves synthetic feasibility and binding affinity, with a 4% increase in drug-likeness and a 6% improvement in synthetic feasibility. Furthermore, by integrating explicit interaction data through a concept-based model, our framework enhances interpretability, offering valuable insights into the molecular design process.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel, fragment-based molecular generation framework tailored for specific proteins, aiming to address limitations of existing methods, such as lack of synthetic feasibility, drug-likeness, and interpretability in atom-level approaches or overlooking comprehensive protein-molecule interaction factors in fragment-based methods. The method employs a two-stage process: (1) constructing a protein subpocket and molecular arm concept-based neural network to integrate interaction force information and geometric complementarity to sample molecular arms for specific protein subpockets; and (2) introducing a diffusion model to generate molecular backbones connecting these arms, ensuring structural integrity and chemical diversity. The authors claim improved synthetic feasibility, binding affinity, drug-likeness, and interpretability, supported by experimental results and comparisons with baseline methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of a concept-based model for arm selection with a diffusion model for scaffold generation. While both concept-based modelling and diffusion models are individually established techniques in molecular generation, their integration within a fragment-based approach, focusing on explicit interaction data and geometric complementarity, represents a unique contribution. Existing fragment-based methods often fall short in explicitly modeling these interaction forces and geometric complementarity, or suffer from atom-level methods' disadvantages in generating molecules with poor synthetic feasibility.
    *The method also contributes a new use of Protein-Ligand Interaction Profiler (PLIP) for sub-pocket generation. The process uses Protein-Ligand Interaction Profiler (PLIP), allowing for a more effective protein-ligand binding structure.*

*   **Significance:** The work attempts to bridge the gap between atom-level and fragment-based molecular generation methods, leveraging the strengths of both. The explicit incorporation of interaction data and geometric complementarity enhances the interpretability of the molecular design process, a crucial aspect often lacking in purely data-driven approaches.  If the claimed improvements in synthetic feasibility, drug-likeness, and binding affinity are reproducible and generalizable across a wider range of targets, this could significantly impact drug discovery workflows. The demonstration of the effectiveness of this method could have a positive impact on the process of developing new drugs, leading to a faster process, more effective drugs, and new opportunities for drugs to be designed.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of current molecular generation methods and articulates a well-defined research problem.
    *   **Integration of Multiple Techniques:** The combination of concept-based modeling and diffusion models provides a sophisticated approach to molecular generation.
    *   **Emphasis on Interpretability:** The concept-based model enhances the interpretability of the arm selection process, offering valuable insights for molecular design.
    *   **Performance Improvements:** Experimental results suggest improvements in key metrics such as drug-likeness, synthetic feasibility, and binding affinity compared to baseline methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The experimental evaluation is conducted on a relatively small dataset (100 novel protein-ligand complexes), which raises questions about the generalizability of the results. It is important to show the results can be transferred to other protein complexes, allowing the process to be used in a real world setting.
    *   **Baseline Comparison:** A more extensive comparison with a wider range of state-of-the-art methods, including more recent fragment-based approaches, would strengthen the claims of superiority.
    *   **Justification of the Concept Layer:** The choice of key factors such as non-polar space occupation and surface complementarity, while intuitive, could benefit from a more rigorous justification or ablation study to demonstrate their relative importance and contribution to performance.
    *   **Impact is uncertain** The impact that the improvements to drug-likeness and synthetic feasibility have needs to be explored. While improvements are stated, there may be an opportunity to discuss how meaningful these improvements are and what type of impact this has on drug discovery.
    *   **Lack of detail about Protein-Ligand Interaction Profiler:** The paper provides little detail on how the Protein-Ligand Interaction Profiler is used for sub-pocket generation and molecular pairs. More detail on this would assist with determining how effective the process is.

*   **Potential Influence:** If validated and generalized, this approach could become a valuable tool for drug discovery, offering a more interpretable and efficient means of generating molecules with desirable properties.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially significant contribution to the field of molecular generation by integrating concept-based modeling and diffusion models within a fragment-based framework. The focus on explicit interaction data and geometric complementarity enhances interpretability and addresses limitations of existing methods. The reported performance improvements are promising.

However, the score is not higher due to concerns about the limited generalizability of the results, the need for a more comprehensive comparison with state-of-the-art methods, and a more rigorous justification of the chosen concepts. Further research and validation are needed to fully assess the impact and robustness of the proposed approach.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### FASIONAD++ : Integrating High-Level Instruction and Information Bottleneck in FAt-Slow fusION Systems for Enhanced Safety in Autonomous Driving with Adaptive Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08162v1)
- **Authors**: Kangan Qian, Ziang Luo, Sicong Jiang, Zilin Huang, Jinyu Miao, Zhikun Ma, Tianze Zhu, Jiayin Li, Yangfan He, Zheng Fu, Yining Shi, Boyue Wang, Hezhe Lin, Ziyu Chen, Jiangbo Yu, Xinyu Jiao, Mengmeng Yang, Kun Jiang, Diange Yang
- **Abstract**: Ensuring safe, comfortable, and efficient planning is crucial for autonomous driving systems. While end-to-end models trained on large datasets perform well in standard driving scenarios, they struggle with complex low-frequency events. Recent Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision Language Models (VLMs) advancements offer enhanced reasoning but suffer from computational inefficiency. Inspired by the dual-process cognitive model "Thinking, Fast and Slow", we propose $\textbf{FASIONAD}$ -- a novel dual-system framework that synergizes a fast end-to-end planner with a VLM-based reasoning module. The fast system leverages end-to-end learning to achieve real-time trajectory generation in common scenarios, while the slow system activates through uncertainty estimation to perform contextual analysis and complex scenario resolution. Our architecture introduces three key innovations: (1) A dynamic switching mechanism enabling slow system intervention based on real-time uncertainty assessment; (2) An information bottleneck with high-level plan feedback that optimizes the slow system's guidance capability; (3) A bidirectional knowledge exchange where visual prompts enhance the slow system's reasoning while its feedback refines the fast planner's decision-making. To strengthen VLM reasoning, we develop a question-answering mechanism coupled with reward-instruct training strategy. In open-loop experiments, FASIONAD achieves a $6.7\%$ reduction in average $L2$ trajectory error and $28.1\%$ lower collision rate.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the FASIONAD++ paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FASIONAD++, a dual-system framework for autonomous driving that integrates a fast end-to-end (E2E) planner with a slow, reasoning-based module powered by Vision-Language Models (VLMs). This approach aims to combine the efficiency of E2E models for routine tasks with the enhanced reasoning capabilities of VLMs for complex or uncertain scenarios. FASIONAD++ employs an adaptive switching mechanism based on real-time uncertainty assessment to activate the slow system only when needed. The framework also includes an information bottleneck to refine VLM inputs, high-level action guidance for strategic planning, and bidirectional knowledge exchange between the fast and slow systems. Experiments demonstrate improvements in safety metrics (collision rate) and trajectory accuracy on several autonomous driving benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of a dual-system approach leveraging both E2E and VLM components is not entirely new, as the paper acknowledges existing dual-process paradigms. The novelty lies in the specific implementation details, particularly:

    *   **Adaptive Switching Mechanism:** The use of real-time uncertainty assessment to dynamically switch between the fast and slow systems is a key differentiating factor. This allows for efficient resource allocation, avoiding the computational overhead of constantly running the VLM-based module.
    *   **Information Bottleneck and High-Level Action Guidance:** The paper's claim that E2E failures often stem from decision-making rather than perception is compelling. The information bottleneck effectively extracts the high-level information to be used for the decisions by the slow system. The integration of VLM feedback as both a scene-derived planning state and as high-level action guidance, impacting both the fast system's decision-making and its trajectory generation, appears novel.
    *   **Planning-oriented QA and VLM Tuning:** The use of visual and BEV prompts to improve the reliability of VLM outputs and the reward-guided training strategy to align the VLM with planning objectives are also valuable contributions.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Safety:**  The focus on improving safety metrics, particularly reducing collision rates, is crucial in autonomous driving.  The demonstrated improvements on nuScenes, Bench2Drive and CARLA, are significant.
    *   **Efficiency:** The adaptive switching mechanism addresses a critical challenge in deploying VLM-based systems in real-time applications: computational cost. By intelligently activating the VLM only when necessary, FASIONAD++ offers a more practical solution.
    *   **Interpretability:**  The paper claims enhanced interpretability, which is important for building trust in autonomous systems. The use of VLMs provides the potential for explaining the reasoning behind decisions, but the paper does not provide enough evidence to support it.
    *   **Generalizability:** The paper demonstrates that the proposed methodology has a broad range applicability by experimenting with different base models and multiple datasets.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear and well-structured paper:** The paper clearly explains the proposed framework, its components, and the experimental setup.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments cover a range of scenarios and benchmarks, providing strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of FASIONAD++.
    *   **Detailed ablation studies:** The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contributions of individual components and design choices.
    *   **Focus on safety:** The emphasis on improving safety metrics is well-aligned with the key challenges in autonomous driving.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited analysis of interpretability:** While the paper mentions the potential for interpretability, it does not provide a detailed analysis of how the VLM-based module can be used to explain decision-making.
    *   **Reliance on Simulated Environments:** The experiments are primarily conducted in simulated environments (nuScenes, CARLA, Bench2Drive). It would be helpful to validate the approach in real-world driving scenarios to assess its robustness and generalizability.
    *   **Limited evaluation of the explainability claims:** While the authors claim increased interpretability through the use of VLMs, there's not a clear demonstration or evaluation of how this interpretability is realized in practice. Real-world driver intervention studies showcasing improved trust or understanding in FASIONAD++'s decisions would strengthen the claim.
    *   **Computational Overhead (Slow system activation):** The paper mentions adaptive switching to reduce computational overheads. The computational cost of uncertainty estimation, as well as the added complexity of the slow system when activated, needs to be properly addressed.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of autonomous driving by effectively integrating E2E and VLM-based approaches. The adaptive switching mechanism, information bottleneck, high-level action guidance, planning-oriented QA, and VLM tuning strategies are innovative and well-justified. The experiments demonstrate significant improvements in safety and accuracy on several autonomous driving benchmarks.

However, the paper has some weaknesses, including the lack of a detailed analysis of interpretability, reliance on simulated environments, and unclear evaluation of the computational and power constraints introduced by the slow system. These limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multimodal Generation of Animatable 3D Human Models with AvatarForge
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.08165v1)
- **Authors**: Xinhang Liu, Yu-Wing Tai, Chi-Keung Tang
- **Abstract**: We introduce AvatarForge, a framework for generating animatable 3D human avatars from text or image inputs using AI-driven procedural generation. While diffusion-based methods have made strides in general 3D object generation, they struggle with high-quality, customizable human avatars due to the complexity and diversity of human body shapes, poses, exacerbated by the scarcity of high-quality data. Additionally, animating these avatars remains a significant challenge for existing methods. AvatarForge overcomes these limitations by combining LLM-based commonsense reasoning with off-the-shelf 3D human generators, enabling fine-grained control over body and facial details. Unlike diffusion models which often rely on pre-trained datasets lacking precise control over individual human features, AvatarForge offers a more flexible approach, bringing humans into the iterative design and modeling loop, with its auto-verification system allowing for continuous refinement of the generated avatars, and thus promoting high accuracy and customization. Our evaluations show that AvatarForge outperforms state-of-the-art methods in both text- and image-to-avatar generation, making it a versatile tool for artistic creation and animation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Multimodal Generation of Animatable 3D Human Models with AvatarForge":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AvatarForge, a novel AI-driven framework for generating animatable 3D human avatars from text or image inputs.  The system combines LLM-based reasoning with off-the-shelf 3D human generators to achieve fine-grained control over body and facial details. A key component is an auto-verification agent that iteratively refines the generated avatars based on input specifications, enhancing accuracy and customization. The system also integrates with motion agents for animating the generated models using natural language commands. Experimental results claim AvatarForge outperforms existing methods in visual quality and customization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of combining LLMs with procedural 3D human generators and an auto-verification loop has some novelty.  It moves beyond direct diffusion-based generation and attempts to leverage the reasoning capabilities of LLMs for finer control.  The "dynamic manual" concept, updated by the auto-verification agent, is also an interesting element.  However, the individual components (LLMs, 3D generators, motion agents) are not novel themselves. The novelty lies primarily in the specific integration and workflow.

* **Significance:** The generation of high-quality, animatable 3D human avatars is a significant challenge with applications in various industries (gaming, film, VR/AR).  A system that can improve the quality, customizability, and ease of use of avatar creation would be a valuable contribution. The claim of outperforming state-of-the-art methods, if substantiated, makes the paper potentially significant. The added feature of animating the generated avatar using natural language, which enhances its practical utility.

* **Strengths:**
    *   **Integration of different components:**  Successfully combines LLMs, 3D generators, and motion control into a coherent framework.
    *   **Auto-verification and iterative refinement:** Addresses the limitations of directly controlling 3D generators using LLMs by incorporating a feedback loop.
    *   **Claims of superior performance:** A significant strength if backed by strong experimental evidence.
    *   **Focus on usability and customization:** The system aims to make 3D avatar creation more accessible and customizable.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on existing tools:** AvatarForge relies heavily on off-the-shelf 3D human generators and motion agents. The paper's contribution is primarily in orchestrating these components, which reduces the core technical innovation.
    *   **Lack of detail on the auto-verification agent:** The paper lacks a thorough explanation of how this agent functions. What specific metrics are used for evaluation? How are discrepancies detected? The implementation details are crucial to assessing the effectiveness of the entire system.
    *   **Quality of the figures:** The generated figures appear to have a cartoonish style that don't look very realistic. A comparison with a state of the art method that provides much more realistic results, for example the LRM method, might not be in the authors advantage.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper contains instances of wordiness and some of the descriptions of the techniques and the dynamic manual can be ambiguous.

*   **Potential Impact:** AvatarForge, if validated by further research and development, could lower the barrier to creating high-quality, personalized 3D avatars. This could have a significant impact on industries that rely on realistic human models. Further, the idea of combining LLMs with procedural generation and auto-verification could be applied to other areas of 3D content creation.

*   **Justification for Score:** While AvatarForge proposes a novel integration of existing techniques and demonstrates a promising approach to 3D avatar generation, it has noticeable areas for improvement. The reliance on existing tools diminishes its core novelty, and the lack of implementation details regarding the auto-verification agent weakens the assessment of its effectiveness. The paper's significance hinges on the claim of outperforming state-of-the-art methods, which needs more rigorous support.
